# Pi sweep review (perf focus) — CastCircle

Exhaustive per-file pass: 277 code files across 148 batches — model ds4/GLM-5.3-Flash-Q2:off — 2026-08-30.

## Findings

- [medium] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:130-142 — migration step failure handler treats any error message containing 'already exists' as benign already-applied and returns normally — a real failure whose message merely contains that substring (e.g. a table/index named with 'already exists' in the error text, or a constraint violation echoing a column name) is swallowed, Drift advances user_version, and the missing column/index is never repaired — every later query on that column dies with 'no such column' forever, and the migration never re-runs — narrow the match to the exact duplicate-column/already-exists error codes or rethrow on non-SQLite-duplicate errors
- [medium] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:172-180 — four index creations run sequentially inside one onUpgrade step, each awaited in turn — on a slow device this serializes four DDL statements behind one another during app startup upgrade path — batch them in a single transaction or issue them concurrently where Drift allows
- [low] lib/data/database/app_database.dart:262-273 — insertRecording opens a transaction and, when productionId/scriptLineId are present, runs a delete-then-insert; the delete predicate matches on (productionId, scriptLineId) but the recordings table's only declared index is idx_recordings_production_line (productionId, scriptLineId) — that index covers the predicate, so this is fine; however the delete is unconditional inside the transaction even when neither field is present, and the guard `entry.productionId.present && entry.scriptLineId.present` means a companion built without those fields silently skips the dedupe delete and piles duplicate rows for the same line — require the companion to always carry both fields or fail loudly instead of silently skipping the dedupe
- [low] lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart:10-13 — CastRole.fromString builds the name map via asNameMap() on every call — for a three-value enum this is negligible, but if fromString is called per-row during cast load (assumed caller not visible), the map is rebuilt each time — hoist to a static final map if profiling shows per-row calls
- [info] lib/data/models/cast_member_model.dart:46 — hasJoined derives joined state from userId != null rather than joinedAt — if callers rely on this to decide whether to re-fetch invitation state, a member with a userId but no joinedAt timestamp is treated as joined with no join time; verify intended semantics against the caller (not visible in this batch)

- [low] lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart:60-62 — cache eviction is FIFO, not LRU: `remove(_gainCache.keys.first)` drops the oldest-inserted entry, so a hot path repeatedly re-analyzed can be evicted while stale entries linger — consequence is repeated MethodChannel loudness analysis (native file decode) for evicted-but-hot paths at scale (many recordings × re-recordings), each costing a full decode round-trip — smallest safe fix: evict the least-recently-used entry (e.g. re-insert on hit, or use a proper LRU structure) instead of `keys.first`.
- [low] lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart:68-70 — `prefetch` fires an unawaited `volumeFor` future; if the same path is prefetched concurrently before the first analysis completes, each call re-runs the native channel invocation (cache is only populated after the await) — consequence is duplicate native decode work when a caller prefetches the same path twice in quick succession (e.g. list-build + immediate play) — smallest safe fix: track in-flight futures per path and return/reuse the pending future in `prefetch`/`volumeFor`.

lib/data/services/analytics_service.dart — clean
lib/data/services/audio_level_service.dart — findings: 2
- [low] lib/data/services/deep_link_service.dart:94 — broadcast StreamController<PendingJoin> is never drained by any visible subscriber and dispose() is only reachable via the singleton's dispose() — if no widget subscribes before init() fires, events accumulate in the broadcast buffer for the app's lifetime; growing input is the number of rejected/valid deep links received per session (unbounded across a long-lived session, though each event is a tiny object) — consequence is a slow, unbounded memory growth of buffered PendingJoin events plus repeated debugPrint/logError work per link — smallest safe fix: use a non-broadcast controller (or ensure a subscriber exists before init) so events are dropped when nobody listens.
- [low] lib/data/services/deep_link_service.dart:119 — _appLinks.uriLinkStream.listen(...) subscription handle is discarded and never cancelled in dispose() — the underlying app_links stream subscription keeps firing _handleUri for the whole process lifetime even after dispose() closes only the controller; each subsequent link still runs regex matching, debugPrint, and logError — consequence is leaked listener doing per-link work after teardown — smallest safe fix: store the StreamSubscription and cancel it in dispose().
- [info] lib/data/services/deep_link_service.dart:132 — debugPrint on every incoming link plus a logError write per rejected link runs on the main isolate; at a scale of many rapid link taps (e.g. testing many malformed invites) each event does synchronous string interpolation, regex compile of _unsafeChars/_codePattern is static so that's fine, but the debugPrint and disk-append via DebugLogService.logError (which does a synchronous File writeAsStringSync per entry, debug_log_service.dart:187) block the UI thread per event — consequence is jank during rapid link handling — smallest safe fix: gate debugPrint behind kDebugMode and batch/async the log append. (Assumption: DebugLogService.logError routes to _appendSync as shown in the inlined context file.)
- [info] lib/data/services/contact_picker_service.dart:28 — pickContact() invokes a platform channel with no timeout; if the native side never replies (e.g. plugin not wired on a beta OS), the Future never completes and the caller's await hangs forever, blocking the pick flow — consequence is an unresponsive picker UI awaiting a dead future — smallest safe fix: add .timeout(const Duration(seconds: 30)) with a cancel/cleanup path. (Assumption: native handler behavior is not visible in this batch.)

- [low] lib/data/services/demo_production_service.dart:119 — whole-asset string load on the demo path — `rootBundle.loadString` reads the entire bundled Hamlet demo text into one String and then `ScriptImportService().importFromText` parses it synchronously on the calling isolate; trigger is once per demo load (rare, user-initiated), input is the fixed bundled asset (bounded by asset size, not user data), work is O(asset bytes) parse, affected resource is the UI isolate during first-run demo setup — consequence is a one-time main-thread hitch of tens of ms on a ~100KB text, only on the demo entry point; smallest safe fix is none needed at this scale (asset is fixed-size and the call is already async via `loadString`), or move `importFromText` behind `compute()` if profiling shows a jank — [info] lib/data/services/demo_production_service.dart:59 — `load()` re-parses the bundled script on every invocation — each call to `DemoProductionService.load` re-runs `_parseBundledScript` (asset read + full text parse) even when `existing != null` (production already open), so a caller that invokes load on every navigation re-does O(asset) parse work; trigger frequency depends on unseen callers (assumed: demo entry button, so rare), growing input is the fixed asset (bounded), consequence is redundant re-parse per invocation; smallest safe fix is memoize the parsed `ParsedScript` in a static field after first successful parse — [low] lib/data/services/demo_production_service.dart:103-113 — SharedPreferences round-trip inside character preselect — `_preselectCharacter` awaits `SharedPreferences.getInstance()` and a get/set each time load() runs; getInstance is cached after first call so cost is small, trigger is once per demo load, consequence negligible; no fix needed (noting only because the checklist asks for per-item I/O — this is one item, not a loop) — [info] lib/data/services/espeak_heteronyms.dart:80-91 — regex rules re-run over every dialogue line — `apply()` runs 5 `replaceAllMapped` passes (each a full-string regex scan) over every line of dialogue before TTS; trigger is per spoken line (high frequency during rehearsal playback), input is one line of dialogue (short, bounded by line length), work is 5 regex scans per line, affected resource is the audio-prep path on the UI/background isolate — consequence is microseconds-to-sub-ms per line, imperceptible; smallest safe fix is none (rules are precompiled static finals, so the "regex compiled per call" class does not apply); if profiling ever shows cost, combine rules into one alternation pass — [info] lib/data/services/espeak_heteronyms.dart:85-88 — `lastIndexOf` inside replace callback — the callback calls `before.lastIndexOf(word)` twice per match to locate the group offset; `before` is the short matched substring (bounded by pattern length), so this is O(match length) not O(line length), trigger per match, consequence negligible; smallest safe fix would be using `m.start`/group offsets directly, but no measurable impact at this scale.

- [medium] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:31-43 — `_onTimings` accumulates every frame's build/raster duration into `_buildMs`/`_rasterMs` for a full 30 s window before `_report` sorts and clears them; between reports the two lists grow unboundedly with frame rate (60 fps → ~1800 doubles each, plus the per-frame `DateTime.now()` at line 39 and the `_reportEvery` comparison on every timing callback) — trigger: every frame timing callback (each frame), growing input: frame count per 30 s window (unbounded if install() is called and the app runs long without a report flush, e.g. if `_report` is skipped when `_buildMs` is empty the window never resets), work per unit: O(1) append + O(n log n) sort at report time, affected resource: UI isolate memory and main-thread time on every frame — smallest safe fix: cap the window (e.g. ring buffer of fixed size or flush when a max count is reached) and hoist the `DateTime.now()`/window check out of the per-frame loop (compute it once per `_onTimings` call, not per timing entry).
- [medium] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:51-52 — `_report` copies and sorts two full-window lists (`List.of(...)..sort()`) on the UI isolate every 30 s; at 60 fps that is ~3600 elements sorted on the main thread, ~O(n log n) with n≈1800 per list, blocking frame scheduling for a few ms each report — smallest safe fix: maintain running p90/p99 via a small fixed-size reservoir or histogram updated in `_onTimings` instead of sorting the whole window at report time.
- [low] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:37 — `_jank` counts frames over 16.7 ms but `_report` divides by `_buildMs.length` (line 55) while `_jank` is incremented per timing entry including raster-only jank; if `timings` batches multiple entries the ratio can exceed 100% or misreport — smallest safe fix: count jank against the same denominator used for `n` (increment `_jank` only for entries appended to `_buildMs`).
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:203,259-274 — `_cachePathFor` computes a SHA-1 over the full UTF-8 text plus voice/speed on every cache miss (and the `utf8.encode` allocates a fresh byte list per call); trigger: every uncached synthesize() call (per spoken line), growing input: text length (unbounded — caller-supplied script line), work per unit: O(text length) hash + allocation, affected resource: UI isolate CPU on the play path right before synthesis — smallest safe fix: memoize the key per (text,voice,speed) in a small LRU map, or hash incrementally over the existing bytes without re-encoding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:291-296 — `_schedulePrune`'s isolate closure `listSync()`s the whole cache directory and `statSync()`s every file, building a full `(File, DateTime, int)` entry list each run; trigger: once per app run when the cache dir is first created, growing input: number of cached WAVs (unbounded across sessions — thousands at steady state per the comment), work per unit: O(n) stat + O(n log n) sort, affected resource: a background isolate's CPU/disk (not UI thread, so not blocking), but the full entry list is allocated even when `total <= maxBytes` short-circuits at line 297 — smallest safe fix: stat-and-accumulate sizes first, and only build/sort the entry list when the total exceeds `maxBytes`.
- [low] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:480-506 — `_writeWav` writes PCM with a per-sample Dart loop (`setInt16` per sample) plus a byte-by-byte `s()` helper for the RIFF header; for a 48 KB/s-of-audio WAV at 24 kHz that is ~48 k setInt16 calls per second of audio on the isolate thread — smallest safe fix: build the PCM via `Float32List`→`Int16List` conversion (bulk view over a `ByteData`) instead of a per-sample loop.
- [low] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:501-505 — `_writeWav` allocates `ByteData(44 + n*2)` sized for the full audio then `writeAsBytesSync` the whole buffer; for long lines this is a single large allocation per synthesis on the isolate — acceptable at current scale (one WAV per request), no fix needed beyond streaming if lines grow to minutes.
- [info] lib/data/services/frame_stats_service.dart:24-29 — `install()` registers `addTimingsCallback` but nothing in the shown file ever removes it or stops the 30 s reporting; if the service is installed per rehearsal session the callback and its growing window lists persist for the process lifetime — verify callers to confirm whether uninstall is missing (assumption: no other file in this batch shows the uninstall path).
- [info] lib/data/services/kokoro_onnx_service.dart:256-257 — `_cacheDirPath`/`_pruneScheduled` are static fields on a singleton that lives for the process; the prune runs "once per app run" by design so the cache can exceed 150 MB within a single long session (prune is never re-scheduled after the first run) — verify intended retention; consequence: unbounded disk growth within one session, smallest safe fix: re-arm `_schedulePrune` after each prune completes or on a timer.

- [medium] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:216-218 — PCM conversion loop allocates a fresh Float32List and a ByteData.sublistView per chunk and runs a per-sample division on the main isolate thread; at 16 kHz a 100 ms chunk is 1600 samples (~1600 divisions + 2 allocations per chunk, ~20 chunks/s during rehearsal) — consequence is steady main-thread CPU churn and allocation pressure during live capture — smallest safe fix: reuse a preallocated Float32List buffer sized to the chunk and reuse the ByteData view, or move the conversion into the decode loop's existing worker isolate (it already runs off-main).
- [medium] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:210-243 — the isolate command loop keeps `stream`, `lastSent`, and `uid` as loop-invariant locals but re-creates `Float32List(12800)` (0.8 s of silence) on every 'end' command; each utterance end allocates ~51 KB that is discarded immediately — consequence is repeated large allocation per utterance (one per script line, so hundreds per session) — smallest safe fix: hoist the silence buffer to a top-level `final` constant outside the `await for` loop.
- [low] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:200-207 — `decodeAndReport` drains `recognizer.isReady` in a while loop and sends a partial on every text change with no rate cap; if the recognizer emits many intermediate results per chunk the SendPort traffic and string rebuilds grow with chunk frequency — consequence is extra isolate-message overhead during long utterances — smallest safe fix: only send when the text differs AND at most once per N ms (throttle), or confirm the recognizer already coalesces (assumption: sherpa-onnx getResult returns cumulative text, which bounds it to one message per decode pass).
- [low] lib/data/services/live_asr_service.dart:111-112 — `ready.future.timeout(30s)` leaves the spawned isolate and ReceivePorts alive if init never completes (the timeout path returns false without calling stop()); a failed init leaks the isolate plus its native model memory until the next stop() — consequence is a leaked background isolate holding loaded ONNX weights after a failed start — smallest safe fix: on timeout, call `stop()` (or kill the spawned isolate) before returning false.
- [low] lib/data/services/media_control_service.dart:33 — `setMethodCallHandler` is registered in activate() but never cleared in deactivate(); repeated activate/deactivate cycles (rehearsal start/end) stack handlers on the same channel — consequence is stale handler invocations and growing native-channel registrations across sessions — smallest safe fix: call `setMethodCallHandler(null)` (or re-set the same handler idempotently) in deactivate().
- [low] lib/data/services/media_control_service.dart:36,56 — `invokeMethod('activate')`/`invokeMethod('deactivate')` are awaited sequentially with no timeout; if the native side never replies the activate/deactivate call hangs the caller's future — consequence is rehearsal start/end blocked on an unresponsive platform channel — smallest safe fix: add a short `.timeout()` or fire-and-forget with a logged failure.

- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:216 — `_states` is a long-lived map that only grows: every model id ever seen (including per-download error/progress keys from the native channel) is retained for the app's lifetime, and `_listeners` (line 217) accumulates `VoidCallback`s with no removal on widget dispose unless callers remember to call `removeListener` — consequence: unbounded memory growth and stale listener invocations (`_notify` at 231-235 fires every retained callback on every progress tick, so a leaked listener keeps rebuilding dead widgets) as downloads accumulate over a long session — smallest safe fix: bound `_states` to the known `availableModels` ids (drop unknown keys on write) and require/verify listener removal in the owning widget's dispose, or switch to a weak listener registry.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:338-373 — `refreshDownloadedStatus` re-runs the full per-model disk probe (existence check, `lengthSync` stat, and for pinned models a full streamed SHA-256 of up to a 164 MB file via `_verifyDownload`→`crypto.sha256.bind(file.openRead())` at 406) on every invocation, with no memoization of a previously-verified (id, size, mtime) result — consequence: each refresh re-hashes gigabyte-scale model files on the main isolate, blocking the UI thread for seconds and re-doing identical work whenever callers refresh repeatedly (e.g. on every settings-screen visit) — smallest safe fix: cache verification results keyed by (path, sizeBytes, lastModified) and skip re-hashing unchanged files.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:455-459 — `downloadLiveAsr` calls `fileProblem(m, File(await _filePath(m)))` per model inside the loop, and `_filePath` (737-744) resolves the documents directory via a platform-channel round trip (`getApplicationDocumentsDirectory`) on the first call per await chain; because `_docsPath` is only memoized after the first await completes, concurrent/sequential per-model calls before memoization each pay the channel round trip — consequence: up to 4 extra platform-channel round trips per `downloadLiveAsr`/`refreshDownloadedStatus` invocation, adding latency on the UI isolate — smallest safe fix: hoist a single `await getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()` above the loop and pass the resolved docs path into the per-model path computation (the `_docsPath ??=` memo already exists at 739; pre-warm it before the loop).
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:695-699 — `downloadAll` awaits `download(m)` sequentially for each of the 6 models; each `download` performs its own free-space preflight (`_freeDiskSpaceBytes` FFI `statvfs` call at 515), tmp-file cleanup, directory creation, and platform-channel `startDownload` invocation one at a time — consequence: total wall time is the sum of all six downloads' setup+network latencies rather than the max, so a full "download all" takes several times longer than necessary on a fresh install — smallest safe fix: `await Future.wait(availableModels.where((m) => m.downloadUrl.isNotEmpty).map(download))` as `downloadLiveAsr` already does at 460, or at minimum parallelize the preflight/setup phase.
- [low] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:795-832 — `_freeDiskSpaceBytes` re-performs `DynamicLibrary.process()`, three `lookupFunction` lookups, two `malloc`s, a `statvfs` syscall, and two `free`s on every call, and it is called once per model inside `download` (515) and once per refresh path — consequence: a few hundred microseconds of FFI setup + syscall per invocation; negligible per call but repeated across `downloadAll`'s six sequential invocations (see 695-699) it adds measurable setup latency — smallest safe fix: memoize the `lookupFunction` results in static finals (the library handle and function pointers are process-lifetime constants) and keep only the per-call `statvfs`/malloc/free.
- [low] lib/data/services/model_download_service.dart:107-214 — `availableModels.where((m) => m.id == modelId).firstOrNull` (263-264) and the same pattern in `delete` (704) do a linear scan of the 6-element const list per lookup — consequence: trivial at the current fixed list size; only becomes a cost if the registry grows — smallest safe fix: none needed at current scale (informational; a `Map` by id would be the fix if the list grows).
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:55-62 — `isKokoroReady` performs 5 sequential `await File(...).exists()` round trips (model, voices.bin, tokens.txt, lexicon-us-en.txt, plus the `modelsDir` resolution) on every call, and it is called from `isAllReady` (129-137), `getKokoroPaths` (74), `downloadKokoro` (110), and `downloadAll`→`downloadKokoro` — consequence: each readiness check costs ~5 async filesystem round trips on the UI isolate; on Android `isAllReady` additionally chains `ModelDownloadService.instance.isLiveAsrReady()` (which itself walks all live_asr models with per-model file stats in `_groupReady`), so a single "is everything ready?" gate can cost 10+ sequential I/O awaits and stall the UI thread's event loop when invoked per frame or per rebuild — smallest safe fix: batch the existence checks with `Future.wait` over the four file paths (they are independent), and memoize the ready result until a download/delete/refresh mutates state.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:88-101 — `_reclaimLegacyKokoro` re-checks and re-deletes the legacy fp32 directory on every `downloadKokoro` call (109) with no "already reclaimed" latch — consequence: each download attempt pays a directory-existence probe and, when the legacy dir is present, a full recursive delete of a ~600 MB tree synchronously on the caller's isolate before the download even starts, repeatedly delaying every retry of a failed download — smallest safe fix: record a one-time "reclaimed" flag (or check a marker file) so the recursive delete runs at most once per install.
- [medium] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:242-268 — `_extractArchiveStreaming` decompresses the entire bz2 archive to a full temp `.tar` on disk (Step 1) and then re-reads that tar to extract (Step 2), i.e. the ~180 MB archive is written and read back in full even though `archive` (257) holds the decoded tar in memory via `TarDecoder().decodeStream` — consequence: doubles disk I/O for the archive (write ~180 MB tar, then stream-read it again) and, because `TarDecoder().decodeStream(tarInput)` is a non-streaming decode into memory, peak memory holds the whole decompressed tar's entry table — on low-memory devices this is the OOM the comment claims to avoid — smallest safe fix: pipe the bzip2 decoder output directly into the tar entry extraction (single pass, no intermediate full tar file), or use a streaming tar reader over the decoder's output stream.
- [low] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:274-364 — `_downloadFile` constructs a fresh `HttpClient()` per invocation (289) and closes it in `finally` (363); it is called once per archive download so the churn is per-download, not per-chunk — consequence: minor socket/TLS setup cost per download; only material if downloads become frequent — smallest safe fix: reuse a module-level `HttpClient` (with `autoUncompress=false` set per request) instead of constructing one per call.
- [low] lib/data/services/model_manager.dart:336-354 — progress callback throttling emits at most one `onProgress` per ~1 MB of received bytes (345-354), which for a 180 MB archive is ~180 callback invocations that each may end in a caller `setState` — consequence: up to ~180 full-widget rebuilds during a single download; acceptable but worth noting the throttle bound is byte-delta-based, not time-based, so a fast connection can still fire many rebuilds in quick succession — smallest safe fix: additionally throttle by wall-clock (e.g. min 100 ms between emissions) in addition to the 1 MB byte delta.

- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:3296-3321 — `_scrollToCurrentLine` schedules a post-frame callback that reads `_currentLineKey.currentContext` and calls `Scrollable.ensureVisible` on every line advance; with `cacheExtent: 3000` (line 1075) the target widget is materialized, but the callback re-runs the full ensureVisible animation (300 ms easeOut) each time `_processCurrentLine`/`_advanceLine`/`_jumpBack`/`_restartScene` fires — trigger frequency is once per line advance (dozens per rehearsal), work per unit is a full scroll animation + layout pass, affected resource is the main thread during playback — consequence is jank/stutter on the scroll animation while TTS audio starts; smallest safe fix is to guard the callback with a check that the scroll position already matches the target (or coalesce via a single pending callback) so repeated advances within the same frame don't queue redundant animations.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:1055-1060 — `_buildScriptView` builds `charIndexByName` (O(characters)) and the per-row `Consumer` closure captures `dialogueLines`/`myCharacter` fresh on every rebuild of the enclosing `build()`; since `build()` re-runs on every provider change (line-index advance, state flip), the entire ListView.builder's `itemBuilder` closures are recreated and every realized row's `Consumer` re-subscribes — trigger frequency is every line advance (dozens per rehearsal), work per unit is O(rows × characters) map construction plus closure churn, affected resource is main-thread allocation and rebuild cost — consequence is a rebuild storm on each advance that scales with realized row count × cast size; smallest safe fix is to hoist `charIndexByName` and the row builder into a memoized helper keyed on `(script, scene, myCharacter)` so the list widget identity is stable across advances.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:1688-1698 — `_getRehearsalLines`'s cue-practice filter loop is O(dialogueLines) per call, but it is called from `_processCurrentLine`, `_prefetchUpcomingOtherLines`, `_onOtherLineFinished`, `_confirmLineMatch`, `_resetSilenceTimer`'s timeout body, `_handleRemoteJumpBack`, `_handleRemoteSkip`, `_handleRemotePlayPause`, `_recordCurrentLineAttempt`, `_saveSession`, `_completeScene`, and `_preRollVoices` — the memo cache (`_rehearsalLinesCache`/`_rehearsalLinesKey`) only hits when `(script, scene.id, myCharacter, mode)` is unchanged, which it is within a scene, so most calls are cache hits; however the cache key uses `identityHashCode(script)` (line 1671), which can collide across different script objects, silently returning a stale filtered list — trigger frequency is every advance, work per unit is O(n) scan on miss, affected resource is CPU on the UI isolate — consequence is occasional wrong-line playback after a script object swap with the same hash; smallest safe fix is to key the cache on the script object identity itself (`_rehearsalLinesScript` field) rather than `identityHashCode`.
- [low] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:3197-3233 — `_saveRehearsalCaptures` iterates `_capturedAudio.entries` doing per-entry `tempFile.existsSync()`, `copy`, `delete`, and a `SyncQueue.instance.enqueue` — trigger frequency is once per scene completion (user-initiated), work per unit is 3 file I/O ops + one enqueue per captured line, affected resource is main isolate with sync file existence checks — consequence is a UI stall proportional to captured-line count (bounded by lines the actor actually recorded, typically <50); smallest safe fix is to hoist the `existsSync` check into the copy's try/catch (copy already throws on missing source) and batch the enqueues.
- [low] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:3252-3255 — `_purgeRehearsalCaptures` calls `File(captured.path).deleteSync()` per entry inside a loop — trigger frequency is once per discard action, work per unit is one sync delete per captured line, affected resource is main isolate — consequence is a short UI freeze proportional to capture count; smallest safe fix is to switch to `delete()` (async) or batch via `Future.wait`.
- [info] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:114 — `_ttsPrefetch` is a `Map<String, List<Future<String?>>>` that grows per line id; the comment says it is pruned on use (`.remove` at line 2084) and cleared on dispose/scene change (lines 600, 698), so the bound is the number of distinct line ids touched per scene — no unbounded growth exhibited in the shown code; no action needed beyond verifying the scene-change clear path actually fires on every scene switch.
- [info] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:181 — `_lineAttempts` is an unbounded `List<LineAttempt>` that grows one entry per attempt (including retries); trigger frequency is one per line attempt, growing input is number of attempts per session with no cap, work per unit is O(1) append, affected resource is session memory — consequence is negligible for a single rehearsal but unbounded across a very long session with many retries; smallest safe fix is to cap the list (e.g. keep last N) or rely on `_restartScene`'s clear (line 2725) which already bounds it per run.

- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:60-62 — `singleLineChars` computed via full-list scan on every `build` (and every setState from `_loadLocales`/locale changes) — O(n) scan + list allocation per rebuild, with no memoization, re-run on every provider update — hoist into a `select`-derived memo or compute once per script version and cache keyed on the script instance.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:253-256 — `_toggleGender` maps over ALL characters and rebuilds the whole `ParsedScript` (lines, scenes, rawText) just to change one character's gender — O(n) copy of every line/scene object per toggle, allocation churn on a user-triggered action that also triggers `scheduleScriptSave` — copy only the changed character and reuse the existing `lines`/`scenes`/`rawText` references (they are already immutable lists).
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:560-574 — `_applyRename` maps over every line and, for multi-character lines, allocates a new list + joined display string per matching line — O(n) full-script rebuild per rename, plus `_rebuildScript` then re-scans all lines again (charCounts loop 735-739) and re-maps all scenes (769-782) — acceptable for a user action, but the double pass over `updatedLines` (once in `_applyRename`, once in `_rebuildScript`) is redundant — fuse the rename scan into `_rebuildScript`'s existing pass.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:735-739 — `_rebuildScript` recomputes `charCounts` by iterating all updated lines on every rename/merge/delete — O(n) per mutation with no incremental update even though only one character's lines changed — maintain counts incrementally or reuse the previous count map minus/plus the affected character.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:769-782 — `_rebuildScript` re-derives every scene's character set by scanning the full line range of each scene after every single-character mutation — O(scenes × lines-per-scene) per edit, redundant since only scenes overlapping the renamed character's lines can change — recompute only affected scenes (those whose range intersects the edited lines).
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:641-669 — `_migrateCastMembers` awaits `notifier.save(...)` sequentially inside a loop over affected cast members — per-item sequential awaits on independent local persistence calls; with k affected members this is k round-trips — batch the saves or `Future.wait` the independent ones.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:647-667 — cloud rename (`supa.renameCastCharacter`) is awaited per member inside the loop — per-item remote I/O that could batch (one rename RPC per cast member); with many members sharing `oldName` this is one network round-trip each — batch into a single RPC accepting a list of member ids, or parallelize with bounded concurrency.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:633-645 — `notifier.loadForProduction(productionId)` re-loads the full cast list from the provider on every rename even though the screen never displayed it — redundant full-collection load per mutation; if the provider already holds the list in memory, read it instead of re-fetching.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart:456-457 — `_showCharacterDetail` calls `linesForCharacter` and `scenesForCharacter` on every tap, each doing a full O(n) scan of all lines/scenes with no caching — fine for occasional taps, but the detail sheet rebuilds these lists on every sheet rebuild; memoize per (script, character) if the sheet rebuilds frequently.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/cloud_sync_dialog.dart:22-61 — `diffScriptLines` builds `localById` (O(n) map) then walks cloud + local — O(n) total, fine; but `matchedLocalIds` is a `Set<String>` built by adding `loc.id` while the removal pass re-checks membership — correct and linear, no finding beyond noting it is already the efficient shape.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/cloud_sync_dialog.dart:73-88 — `showCloudSyncDialog` computes the full diff and all four counters synchronously on the UI isolate before showing the dialog — for large scripts (thousands of lines) this is O(n) CPU work on the main thread at dialog-open time, blocking first paint — defer the diff computation until after the dialog frame (e.g. compute in `builder` with a `FutureBuilder`/post-frame callback) or move to an isolate for scripts above a few thousand lines.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/cloud_sync_dialog.dart:284-291 — `_sameList` does an element-by-element comparison on every unchanged-candidate line pair — O(m) per line where m = ensemble size; with long ensemble lists across thousands of lines this is O(n·m) in the diff pass — acceptable at current scale; could short-circuit by first comparing `identical` (already done) or hashing, but no bound is exceeded.

## Coverage
lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart — findings: 4
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:118-119 — `_memoFilteredLines`/`_memoLowOcrCount` are called unconditionally on every `build`, and each memo's cache key includes mutable filter state (`_selectedCharacter`, `_showDirections`, `_showLowConfidenceOnly`), so every filter toggle or reorder-mode setState invalidates the cache and re-runs the full O(n) line scan plus a second full O(n) scan for the low-OCR count — two full-list passes per rebuild at n = script line count (hundreds to thousands of lines for a full play) — consequence: every chip toggle, reorder drag, or line selection triggers two linear scans plus list materialization on the UI thread, jank on large scripts — smallest safe fix: compute the low-OCR count once per script identity (key only on `script.lines` identity, not per-build) and derive `filteredLines` lazily only when the filter state actually changed, or fold the count into the single `_filteredLines` pass.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:572-574 — `_memoLowOcrCount` re-walks `script.lines` with a `.where(...).length` on every cache miss even though `_filteredLines` (line 583-599) already walks the same list in the same build; the count could be derived from that single pass — consequence: doubles the per-rebuild linear work over all lines for a value used only by one chip label — smallest safe fix: compute `lowOcrCount` inside `_filteredLines`'s existing loop and return both from one memoized call.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:619-620 — `_reorderLines` does `allLines.indexWhere((l) => l.id == movedLine.id)` and a second `indexWhere` for the target line, i.e. two O(n) linear searches per reorder gesture, on top of the O(n) `removeAt`/`insert` (each O(n) on a `List`) and the O(n) `copyWith(orderIndex:)` reindex loop at 632-634 — consequence: each drag step during reorder mode costs ~5 full-list passes plus a fresh `ParsedScript` allocation; with hundreds of lines and continuous dragging this stutters the UI thread — smallest safe fix: build a `Map<String,int>` id→index once per reorder session (or per `_reorderLines` call) and use it for both lookups; reuse the existing list instead of `toList()` copies.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:631-634 — the reindex loop allocates a fresh `ScriptLine` via `copyWith(orderIndex: i)` for every line on every reorder step, even for lines whose `orderIndex` did not change — consequence: O(n) object allocations and a new `ParsedScript` per drag tick, GC churn during continuous dragging — smallest safe fix: only reassign `orderIndex` for lines between `fromIdx` and `insertAt` whose index actually changed, or reuse the existing line objects when the index is unchanged.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:1146-1157 — `_changeLineType` maps over ALL script lines with `copyWith`-free passthrough but still allocates a new list via `.toList()` and then `_rebuildScript` (line 1291) re-walks all updated lines to recount characters, re-sorts them, and allocates a new `ParsedScript` — consequence: changing one line's type costs O(n) map + O(n) recount + O(n log n) sort + full state replacement, for a single-line edit — smallest safe fix: update only the target line in place (single-element list copy) and incrementally adjust the affected character's count instead of recounting all lines.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:1338-1343 — `_updateLine` maps over all lines and allocates a new list, then recounts ALL characters (1346-1351), rebuilds the gender map (1354-1356), sorts all character entries (1358-1360), and allocates a new `ParsedScript` — all to change one line's text/character — consequence: every "Save" press in the edit sheet or detail panel costs multiple O(n) passes plus a sort on the UI thread; with a large cast this is noticeable per keystroke-adjacent save — smallest safe fix: patch the single changed line and adjust only the two affected characters' counts (decrement old char, increment new char) rather than recounting the whole script.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:1231-1238 — `_applySplit` builds `updatedLines` by copying every line (one new list entry per existing line) and then `_rebuildScript` recounts/sorts all characters again — consequence: one split costs O(n) copy + O(n) recount + O(n log n) sort for a two-line change — smallest safe fix: splice only the affected line (insert the new line after the original) and update counts incrementally.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:1286-1288 — `_deleteLine` filters the whole line list into a new list and `_rebuildScript` recounts all characters/sorts again — consequence: each delete is O(n) filter + O(n) recount + O(n log n) sort + full `ParsedScript` replacement for removing one line — smallest safe fix: remove the single element (`removeAt` on the mutable list) and decrement the character's count.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:1291-1331 — `_rebuildScript` is the shared root cause for the four sites above: on every single-line mutation it (a) walks all updated lines to build `charCounts`, (b) allocates `existingGenders` map over all characters, (c) sorts all character entries, and (d) constructs a fresh `ParsedScript` — consequence: every edit/delete/split/type-change pays O(n) + O(n log n) work and a full state object replacement, which also invalidates `_filteredCache`/`_lowOcrCache` and forces the next build to re-run both full-line scans — smallest safe fix: make `_rebuildScript` accept a patch (changed line(s) + delta counts) and update the existing `ParsedScript` incrementally instead of reconstructing it from scratch.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:1247-1253 — `_lowOcrLines()` re-walks the entire script line list on every invocation, and it is called repeatedly inside the edit sheet's walk-through handlers (lines 938, 979, 1000) — each "Remove"/"Looks right" press re-scans all lines just to find the next flagged line — consequence: O(n) scan per button press inside a modal sheet; with many flagged lines and a large script this adds latency to each step of the walk-through — smallest safe fix: compute the flagged list once when the sheet opens and pass it (or a cached getter keyed on `script.lines` identity) into the sheet's callbacks.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:939,984,1004 — `flagged.indexWhere((l) => l.id == current.id)` and `remaining[...]` lookups are O(flagged) per press; combined with the full `_lowOcrLines()` rescan above this is O(n) per walk-through step — consequence: minor, since flagged count is typically small, but it compounds with the rescan — smallest safe fix: track the current index as an int during the walk instead of searching by id each time.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:110-113 — `charColors` map is rebuilt on every `build` (one entry per character) even though characters only change on script rebuilds — consequence: O(c) map allocations per rebuild; negligible for typical cast sizes but redundant work that the memoized filtered-lines path already tries to avoid — smallest safe fix: memoize `charColors` alongside the script identity (e.g. an Expando or a field keyed on `script.characters` identity).
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:289-305 — the character filter chips list uses `...script.characters.map(...)` inside a `ListView(children:)` constructor, materializing a widget per character eagerly rather than `ListView.builder` — consequence: O(c) widget allocations on every rebuild of the chip row; fine for small casts, wasteful for large ones — smallest safe fix: switch to `ListView.builder` with `itemCount: script.characters.length + 2`.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:452,830 — `File(pdfPath).existsSync()` is a synchronous filesystem stat called during `build`/widget construction (detail panel and edit-sheet builder) — consequence: a blocking disk I/O syscall on the UI thread every time the detail panel or edit sheet is (re)built; on slow network-backed storage (iOS Documents container after reinstall) this can add milliseconds of main-thread stall per rebuild — smallest safe fix: resolve existence once in `_resolvePdfPath` (async) and cache the boolean, or use a non-blocking check with a future/`existsAsync` before showing the viewer.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:94 — `_effectivePdfPath` also calls `File(stored).existsSync()` synchronously, invoked from `build`-adjacent paths (`_buildDetailPanel` line 451, `_editLine` line 827) — consequence: same blocking-stat class as above, one extra syscall per call site per rebuild — smallest safe fix: cache the resolved path/existence result in state after `_resolvePdfPath` completes and consult only the cached value.
- [info] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:156 — `await Future.delayed(const Duration(milliseconds: 300))` before computing the share origin is a fixed artificial delay on the share action's hot path — consequence: 300 ms added latency to every "Share Link" press; not a scaling issue, listed for completeness — smallest safe fix: use `WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback` or await the route pop future instead of a timed delay.
- [info] lib/features/script_editor/script_editor_screen.dart:1434-1440 — `_export` writes the export file with `writeAsString` (not `writeAsStringSync`) and shares from the written path; the write is async and awaited, so no blocking issue; the export content is built in memory (ScriptExporter calls) which is O(script size) but bounded by the script itself — no actionable defect beyond noting the exporter cost is inherent to the feature — smallest safe fix: none needed here.

## Coverage
ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift — clean
ios/Runner/AppleSttPlugin.swift — findings: 4
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:232 — `lastProgressEmit` dictionary keyed by modelId is never pruned; every completed/cancelled/failed download leaves an entry forever — after hundreds of downloads over the app's lifetime the map grows unbounded and every progress callback does a dictionary lookup on an ever-larger map — smallest safe fix: remove the entry for `modelId` in `didFinishDownloadingTo`/`didCompleteWithError` (or on `removeDownloadRecord`) so the map holds only in-flight downloads.
- [medium] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:67-75 — `persistDownloadRecord`/`removeDownloadRecord` read-modify-write the entire persisted records dictionary from UserDefaults on every start/cancel/finish; with N downloaded models this is O(N) deserialization+serialization per download event, and UserDefaults writes are synchronous property-list serialization on the main thread (delegate queue is `.main`, line 44) — at hundreds of models this adds main-thread blocking work to each download start/finish — smallest safe fix: keep the in-memory `records` dict cached and mutate only the changed key, or move persistence off the main queue.
- [low] ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift:181 — `pow(2.0, Double(retryCount))` recomputed per retry where a small lookup table would do; negligible cost, only 6 retries — smallest safe fix: none needed / precomputed constants (informational only, not counted as a real issue).

## Coverage
ios/Runner/AudioAnalysisPlugin.swift — clean
ios/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift — findings: 2
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:51-85 — `synthesize` spawns a fresh `Task` per method call and hops to `MainActor.run` twice per request (begin + end background task) plus a `DispatchQueue.main.async` result hop; each synthesis request pays main-thread scheduling overhead and the background-task bookkeeping is re-created per call — at scale of repeated TTS calls (e.g., batch narration of many lines) this adds per-request main-thread churn and leaves no reuse of the task structure — smallest safe fix: hoist the background-task begin/end into a reusable helper or reuse a single long-lived `Task`/actor for synthesis requests instead of spawning a new `Task` per call.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:26-40 — `loadModel` spawns a fresh `Task` per method call with no guard against concurrent duplicate loads; repeated `loadModel` calls while a load is in flight spawn parallel `loadModel()` work (model download/inference setup) — at scale of a user tapping "load" repeatedly or a UI retry loop this duplicates expensive model-load work and can thrash memory/GPU — smallest safe fix: guard with an in-flight flag or reuse a single load task so concurrent calls coalesce.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:87-88 — `getVoices` returns `KokoroMLXService.availableVoices` synchronously per request; if that static is a computed/rebuilt collection per call (not visible here), each request re-pays construction — smallest safe fix: confirm `availableVoices` is a precomputed static constant; if so no change needed.
- [low] ios/Runner/ContactPickerPlugin.swift:48-62 — `topViewController` walks `presentedViewController` chain per pick request; this is O(depth) per user-initiated pick, not a hot loop — no finding beyond noting it is bounded by UI depth.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift:59-62 — the background-task expiration handler calls `endBackgroundTask` and sets `bgTask = .invalid` from inside the handler; if the expiration handler fires while the synthesis `Task` is still running, the task identifier is invalidated but the later `await MainActor.run` block at 80-84 checks `bgTask != .invalid` on the same variable — this is a correctness/leak shape (double-end or missed end) rather than a performance defect; flagging only because the same variable is mutated across actor hops without synchronization — smallest safe fix: capture the identifier in a local and end it exactly once, or use a dedicated actor for the bookkeeping.

## Coverage
ios/Runner/ContactPickerPlugin.swift — findings: 0
ios/Runner/KokoroMLXPlugin.swift — findings: 4
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:337-367 — pruneCacheIfNeeded() re-runs the full directory enumeration + sort + removal pass on the main synthesize path whenever the cache exceeds 200MB — every synthesize() call after the cap pays an O(n) directory scan (contentsOfDirectory over all cached WAVs, resourceValues per file, full sort by date) before synthesis can proceed; at thousands of cached clips this adds seconds of disk I/O per synthesis request — smallest safe fix: keep the one-shot `pruneScheduled` latch but also reset it only when total ≤ lowWater (or run the prune pass on a background queue kicked once per launch rather than synchronously before each cache lookup), so the scan happens at most once per launch instead of per call.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:322-332 — cacheURL() computes a SHA-256 digest and hex-encodes it on every synthesize() call (twice per miss: line 172 and line 248) — SHA-256 over the full text plus a per-byte `map { String(format:) }` hex loop is cheap for short lines but is repeated work per request where the digest could be memoized per (text,voice,speed) — smallest safe fix: hoist the digest computation into a small memo dictionary keyed by the cache key, or compute the hex via `digest.compactMap` once; impact is microseconds per call, so only worth doing if synthesize() is called at high frequency.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroMLXService.swift:121 — `voices` is loaded into memory as a dictionary of MLXArray embeddings from voices.npz and never bounded or evicted until unloadModel()/deleteModel(); if the NPZ grows (new voices added to the package) the whole voice table stays resident for the process lifetime — smallest safe fix: verify the NPZ size is bounded by the shipped model package; if it is, no action needed.

- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertEncoder.swift:31-34 — loop-invariant group-index arithmetic recomputed per hidden layer; `numHiddenLayers / numHiddenGroups` divisor and groupIdx mapping recomputed on every iteration of the forward pass — consequence: negligible for a fixed small config (numHiddenLayers/numHiddenGroups are compile-time constants of the model), but the divisor is re-evaluated each of ~N layer iterations on every inference call; smallest safe fix: hoist `let layersPerGroup = config.numHiddenLayers / config.numHiddenGroups` above the loop and compute `groupIdx = i / layersPerGroup` — trigger frequency: every TTS inference call; growing input: none (fixed config); work per unit: one integer division per layer; affected resource: CPU on inference thread; observable consequence: none measurable at current scale — reported at info because the checklist item "loop-invariant work not hoisted" is exhibited but the scale threshold is not met.
- [info] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Albert/AlbertLayer.swift:30-33 — whole-range in-place assign of full weight/bias tensors per layer construction — consequence: each AlbertLayer copies its full LayerNorm weight and bias (hiddenSize floats each) during model init; with numHiddenLayers × numHiddenGroups layers this is O(layers × hiddenSize) copies once at model load, not per inference; smallest safe fix: none needed — this is one-time init cost, not a hot path; reported at info only because the assign is a full-tensor copy rather than a zero-copy cast, but it is bounded by model size and executed once.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/ConvWeighted.swift:143-147,172-176 — conditional transpose re-evaluated per forward — when `x.shape.last != weight.shape.last` and `groups == 1`, `weight.transposed()` is re-executed on every call instead of being cached alongside the normalized weight; the transpose is loop-invariant with respect to the frozen weights — smallest safe fix: cache the (possibly transposed) weight once with the normalized weight so the branch reduces to a single precomputed tensor read.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/BuildingBlocks/LSTM.swift:141-160 — same per-timestep allocation/retention pattern in the backward direction: `allCell.append`/`allHidden.append` retain every intermediate until the reversed `MLX.stacked` at lines 163-165, so the backward pass holds the full O(seqLen × hiddenSize) tensor set simultaneously with the forward pass's retained set (both directions run on the same `input`), doubling peak memory for long sequences with no cap on seqLen — smallest safe fix: evaluate/free intermediates per step or stack incrementally rather than accumulating all timesteps before one final stack.

- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:142 — `totalWsquared` materializes a full-length concatenated array by repeating `wSquared` `t/winLen` times (t = signal length in samples), then slices only `outputStride.shape[0]` elements per phase-iteration — allocation churn proportional to audio duration inside the iSTFT hot path — consequence: for a 10 s clip at 24 kHz with winLen 800/hopLen 200, that is ~2400 repeats of an 800-float tensor (~7.7 MB) built and discarded per inverse transform, per utterance, on the inference path — smallest safe fix: compute the per-phase window sum directly as `wSquared` repeated only over the needed slice (or precompute a single `wSquared` and use `MLX.asStrided`/tiling sized to `outputStride.shape[0]`), avoiding the full-length `Array(repeating:)` concat.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:146-171 — the iSTFT builds `windowModLen` (= winLen/hopLen, 4) full-signal-length zero-padded copies of `output` and `windowSum` in lists, then reduces them with element-wise `+=` in a second loop — each `outputs[i]`/`windowSums[i]` is a fresh O(t) allocation and each `+=` in lines 166–171 re-allocates the O(t) accumulator per iteration — consequence: ~8 O(t) temporaries plus 2×(windowModLen−1) O(t) reallocations per inverse STFT call; grows linearly with audio duration and is re-done for every batch element in `inverse()` (line 232–253 loops over `magnitude.shape[0]`) — smallest safe fix: allocate one O(t) output buffer and one O(t) window-sum buffer, scatter-add each phase slice into them in a single pass, then divide once.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:205-224 — `transform()` loops over the batch dimension and calls `mlxStft` per element, appending to `magnitudes`/`phases` lists, then stacks — each per-element call re-pads, re-strides and re-runs the FFT independently instead of batching the strided frames into one `rfft` — consequence: B× separate FFT dispatches and B× window/pad allocations per `callAsFunction`; for typical single-utterance B=1 the overhead is one extra Python/Swift loop pass, but for batched synthesis it multiplies allocation churn and kernel launches linearly with batch size — smallest safe fix: build the strided frames for the whole batch (strided over axis 0 as well) and issue one `MLXFFT.rfft` over the batched frames, eliminating the per-element loop.
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:232-253 — `inverse()` loops over `magnitude.shape[0]` calling `mlxIstft` per element and accumulating `reconstructed` list before stacking — same per-element dispatch pattern as `transform()`: each element re-derives `w`, `totalWsquared`, and the phase-split loop independently — consequence: linear-in-batch duplicate window/overlap-add work and B× O(t) temporaries per call — smallest safe fix: batch the istft by striding the irfft output over the batch axis and performing the overlap-add once for the whole batch.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:59-69,118-128 — `window` is typed `Any` and re-dispatched through `as? String`/`as? MLXArray` plus `lowercased()` on every `mlxStft`/`mlxIstft` call, and `hanning(length:)` is recomputed from scratch each call even though the window depends only on constructor-constant `winLength`/`filterLength` — consequence: a small constant-cost recompute per transform/inverse call; negligible for one-off synthesis but pure repeated work on every utterance — smallest safe fix: compute the hann window once in `MLXSTFT.init` and pass the `MLXArray` (or cache it in a `let`) instead of re-deriving per call.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/MLXSTFT.swift:36-38,40-43 — `unwrap()` performs several full-shape element-wise passes (`%`, `where`, `cumsum`, concatenated) over the phase tensor per batch element — consequence: ~6 O(t) kernel passes per element per inverse call; constant per call but multiplied by batch size in `inverse()`'s loop — smallest safe fix: none required for correctness; if profiling shows it, fuse the modulo/where chain or hoist the per-element loop into a batched op (same fix as the batch-loop finding).
- [medium] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/SineGen.swift:45-47 — `_f02sine` allocates a fresh `MLXRandom.normal([f0.shape[0], f0.shape[2]])` tensor and then does an in-place slice-assign (`radValues[...] = radValues[...] + randIni`) on every call — the random draw is O(T×dim) new memory per invocation with no reuse — consequence: per synthesis call, a full-length Gaussian tensor is generated and consumed once; grows linearly with f0 length (audio duration) and is re-done for every utterance — smallest safe fix: none needed if this noise is intentional per-call; if determinism/perf matter, pre-generate the noise buffer once per model instance (or per fixed chunk size) and slice it, avoiding a fresh O(T) allocation per call.
- [low] ios/Runner/KokoroVendored/Decoder/SineGen.swift:49-60 — `interpolate(...)` is invoked twice per `_f02sine` call with transposes around each — each transpose/interpolate pair materializes O(T×dim) temporaries — consequence: 4 O(T×dim) temporaries per call on the generator hot path; linear in audio duration — smallest safe fix: none required; if profiled hot, fuse the two interpolate steps or operate on the already-transposed layout to avoid the extra `.transposed` materializations.

- [medium] ios/Runner/MisakiVendored/English/FallbackNetwork/BARTDecoderLayer.swift:54-55 — cache concatenation allocates a fresh K/V buffer every decode step — `concatenated([cache.k, k], axis: 2)` copies the entire cached prefix plus the new step into a new MLXArray on each `step()` call, so per-token cost grows linearly with sequence length (O(n²) total allocation/copy across a generation of n tokens) — consequence: incremental decode slows down as the prefix grows; at a few thousand tokens each step re-copies megabytes of K/V on the main thread, adding seconds of cumulative latency to CastCircle's on-device transcription/translation fallback path — smallest safe fix: grow a preallocated K/V cache buffer in-place (write the new step's k/v into the tail of a fixed-capacity buffer and pass a slice/view) instead of re-concatenating the whole history per step.

I'll start by reviewing the two migration files.

**File 1: supabase/migrations/20260314130000_fix_cast_members_rls.sql**

This is a SQL migration file that fixes RLS (Row Level Security) policy recursion issues. Let me analyze it for performance concerns:

- Lines 5-11: Dropping and creating policies — these are DDL operations, not hot-path queries.
- Lines 13-21: Creates a policy using a subquery `select 1 from public.productions where productions.id = cast_members.production_id and productions.organizer_id = auth.uid()`. This is a correlated subquery inside an RLS policy. For performance, this matters when rows are evaluated — the policy is evaluated per-row on reads of `cast_members`. The relevant index would be on `productions(id, organizer_id)` — the primary key on productions covers `id`, and organizer_id would need to be checked. Actually, since `productions.id` is the primary key, the lookup is by PK, which is indexed. So the correlated subquery is a PK lookup per row — acceptable.
- Lines 26-35: Creates a policy on `productions` that includes `exists (select 1 from public.cast_members where cast_members.production_id = productions.id and cast_members.user_id = auth.uid())`. This is a correlated subquery on `cast_members`. - [low] test/espeak_heteronyms_test.dart:59-67 — subprocess spawned per item in test loops — the `ipa()` helper shells out to `espeak-ng` via `Process.runSync` and is invoked once per line inside the `for` loops at lines 77-88 and 97-103 (plus the precondition check at line 83), so each iteration pays full process spawn + exec + stdout parse cost; with the loop bodies at lines 86 and 98/102 each line triggers two sequential `ipa()` calls (raw and rewritten), doubling the round-trips. Trigger frequency: every test run on developer machines/CI where espeak-ng is installed; growing input: the hardcoded line lists (currently 4-6 entries, bounded by literals, so no unbounded growth); work per unit: one synchronous subprocess spawn (~tens of ms each) on the test-runner thread; affected resource: test wall-clock time and process table churn; realistic observable consequence: the espeak-ng agreement group runs ~10-14 sequential spawns, adding noticeable seconds to CI and blocking the test runner synchronously (`runSync`); smallest safe fix: hoist a single `ipa()` result per line by computing raw and rewritten IPA once per loop entry and reusing, or batch all lines into one `espeak-ng` invocation (it accepts multi-line stdin) and split the output — no per-item spawn.

Note on severity/reachability: this is test-only code (MEMBER role, `test/` path), so reachability is capped at low per the severity rules; the defect class is "subprocess spawned per item" from the checklist, and the bound is finite (literal lists), so it stays low rather than medium. The `saysLong`/`ipa` helpers are local to this file; no production symbol is asserted against a constant, so no test-cannot-fail finding applies. The second file's tests assert pure in-memory logic (`shouldReuseLoadedScript`) with no I/O, no loops over unbounded data, and no subprocess work — clean.

## Coverage
lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart — findings: 7
lib/data/services/script_export.dart — findings: 3
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:132-146 — per-line `_findSourcePageFrom` rescans raw lines from a forward cursor with `contains` on both sides — for a long play (N parsed lines × M raw lines within the unbounded tail after the cursor) this is O(N·M) string scans per import; consequence: multi-second import stalls on large scanned scripts even on the "fast" PDFKit path — smallest safe fix: bound the forward scan window (e.g. stop after ~150 raw lines past the cursor, mirroring the bounded `searchWindow` already used in `parseAndMapOcr` at line 601) and return null on miss.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:746-754 — `_findSourcePageFrom` has no window bound at all: the loop runs to `rawLines.length` from `startIndex`, so one unmatched parsed line makes every subsequent scan walk the whole remaining document — consequence: quadratic tail behavior on the native-PDFKit mapping path (same class the bounded window at line 601 fixed for the OCR path) — smallest safe fix: cap the scan at `startIndex + searchWindow` and return null beyond it.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:648-653 — confidence accumulation loop calls `matches(rawLines[i], searchText)` per raw line with full `contains`/`substring` work inside the per-parsed-line closure — consequence: repeated regex-free but O(len) string comparisons per line, multiplied across ~1187+ lines per import — smallest safe fix: hoist the normalized `searchText` length check and early-exit on the first non-matching non-empty line (already done) plus skip the `matches` call when `rawLines[i].length < 8 && searchText.length < 8`.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:449-516 — ML Kit fallback path renders each page to PNG bytes and writes a single reused temp file per page (`writeAsBytes` per iteration) — consequence: per-page disk write + PNG encode on the UI isolate for a 60-page scanned play (~60 sequential round-trips, seconds of blocking I/O) — smallest safe fix: keep the in-memory `byteData` and feed `InputImage.fromBytes` instead of round-tripping through a temp file per page.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:436-439 — `Pdfrx.getCacheDirectory` override is re-assigned on every OCR import call — consequence: redundant async closure allocation per import (negligible per call, but it also re-runs `getTemporaryDirectory()` each time instead of caching the resolved path) — smallest safe fix: memoize the resolved directory path in a field and assign the override once.
- [info] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:194-198 — `Isolate.run` closure captures `vocab` and re-attaches it via `setTheatricalVocab` inside the worker isolate — consequence: the ~251K-word vocabulary is serialized/copied into the isolate on every scored import (allocation churn proportional to vocab size per import) — smallest safe fix: load the vocab inside the worker isolate once and cache it there, or pass a transferable typed representation; verify actual copy cost before treating as more than info.

- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:177-198 — every queue mutation (enqueue, persist after each upload attempt, restore) re-serializes the entire pending+failed job list to JSON and rewrites the whole queue file via temp+rename — trigger: each enqueue/upload-failure/retry transition; growing input: pending+failed job count, unbounded across a long offline session; work per unit: O(n) jsonEncode + full-file write per mutation → O(n²) cumulative I/O as the queue grows; affected resource: disk writes on the UI isolate during recording; consequence: recording many lines offline makes each new enqueue progressively slower and stalls the record flow; smallest safe fix: batch persists (debounce/coalesce with a short timer) or append-only journal instead of full rewrite per mutation.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:246-252 — restore loop calls File(job.localPath).existsSync() per restored job on the main isolate at startup — trigger: once per app start; growing input: persisted job count (unbounded if a previous run queued many); work per unit: one synchronous filesystem stat per job; affected resource: main-thread startup latency; consequence: a large persisted queue delays first paint/app start; smallest safe fix: stat files asynchronously (File.exists()/toFuture) or batch-check via directory listing.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:370-455 — _processQueue loop calls file.existsSync()/lengthSync() synchronously per job inside the upload loop — trigger: per pending job per processing pass; growing input: pending queue length, unbounded; work per unit: synchronous stat per job; affected resource: main isolate during upload processing; consequence: many queued jobs block the UI thread between awaits; smallest safe fix: use async File APIs (exists()/length()).
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:242-245 — queuedKeys set built from both lists on every restore, then re-scanned per restored job — trigger: per restore; growing input: pending+failed count; work per unit: O(n) build + O(n) scan; affected resource: startup CPU; consequence: negligible at realistic queue sizes; smallest safe fix: none needed (already linear; noted only because the comment claims quadratic — the code is linear).
- [info] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:159-163 — _persistPath() re-resolves getApplicationSupportDirectory() on every persist call instead of caching the resolved path — trigger: every persist mutation; growing input: none (constant per call); work per unit: one async platform-channel round-trip; affected resource: main isolate during recording; consequence: adds latency to each queue write; smallest safe fix: cache the resolved path in a field after first resolution (as _tempDir does in tts_service).
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:105-122 — setLocale() fetches the full system voice list via platform channel and re-filters it on every locale change, and assignVoice() (284-343) re-runs the same locale/gender filtering per character assignment — trigger: per locale change / per character assignment; growing input: system voice list (device-dependent, typically dozens); work per unit: full list scan + platform-channel round-trip per call; affected resource: main isolate during rehearsal setup; consequence: repeated channel round-trips and list scans when assigning many characters; smallest safe fix: cache the filtered voice pool per locale (memoize on locale string) and reuse it in assignVoice.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:542-617 — _splitTextForKokoro compiles no regexes per call (static finals) but re-runs the full multi-pass chunking (sentence split → clause split → word split) on every speak() and every prepareKokoro() call for identical text — trigger: per speak()/prefetch of the same line; growing input: line length (bounded ~510-token chunks, but full text unbounded); work per unit: O(text length) with three passes; affected resource: CPU on main isolate per rehearsal line; consequence: redundant recomputation when the same line is re-spoken or prefetched then spoken; smallest safe fix: memoize split results keyed by (text, voice, speed) in a small LRU map.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:229-234 — _playerStateSub subscription registered in init() and only cancelled in dispose() (935) — if dispose() is never called on the singleton (no caller visible in this batch), the stream subscription lives for the app lifetime — trigger: app lifetime; growing input: none; work per unit: one listener; affected resource: minor retained memory; consequence: negligible; smallest safe fix: ensure dispose() is invoked on teardown or use a scoped subscription.
- [low] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:837-842 — prepareKokoro() attaches a Future.wait(...).then(...) diagnostic that holds all chunk futures until every one resolves even though the result is only logged — trigger: per prefetch call; growing input: chunk count per line (bounded by 510-token split, typically <10); work per unit: one future per chunk retained; affected resource: transient allocations; consequence: negligible; smallest safe fix: drop the diagnostic wait or log per-chunk as each resolves.

- [info] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:96 — `writeAsBytes(flush: true)` forces an fsync of the full output on the caller's isolate — for a large WAV this blocks the event loop for the whole write duration (tens of MB) — smallest safe fix: write without `flush: true` (the OS close/flush at app exit is sufficient for a temp file) or use `File.openWrite` with buffered writes.

- [medium] ios/Runner/PdfTextPlugin.swift:80-86 — extractText builds the full text by appending every page's string to an array and joining with "\n" separators; for a large PDF (hundreds/thousands of pages) each page's text is retained and the final join allocates a copy of the entire document text plus separator copies, and the whole string is then shipped over the method channel in one result — memory spike and channel-message size grow unbounded with page count — stream or chunk the result (e.g. per-page events or a file-backed return) instead of one monolithic string
- [medium] ios/Runner/PdfTextPlugin.swift:120-126 — extractTextPerPage loops over all pages calling document.page(at:)?.string and appending to `pages`, with no cap on page count; the returned array of per-page strings plus the pageCount echo is materialized in full before the single result callback — for very large PDFs this is O(pages) CPU + memory on a background queue and one huge channel payload — bound the extraction (paginate/limit the page range) or return lazily
- [low] ios/Runner/PdfTextPlugin.swift:62,105,146 — each method invocation spawns a fresh DispatchQueue.global async block and re-opens the PDF from disk (PDFDocument(url:)) with no memoization of the document across the three sibling calls (extractText, extractTextPerPage, hasEmbeddedText often run in sequence on the same path); repeated parse of the same large PDF costs O(pages) disk+parse per call — cache the opened PDFDocument keyed by path (with invalidation) or have callers batch the decision
- [info] ios/Runner/PdfTextPlugin.swift:160-166 — hasEmbeddedText caps the text probe at 3 pages (min(3, pageCount)), so the per-call work is bounded; no unbounded growth here — no fix needed

## Coverage
macos/Runner/BackgroundDownloadPlugin.swift — findings: 2
macos/Runner/MainFlutterWindow.swift — clean
- [medium] macos/Runner/PdfTextPlugin.swift:73-79 — extractText loads every page's full text into memory and joins it into one giant String before returning — for a large scanned PDF (hundreds of pages, each page's extracted text potentially tens of KB) the accumulated `pageTexts` array plus the `joined(separator:)` result can reach hundreds of MB, and the join copies the whole corpus again; work is O(total text) with an extra full copy, all on a background thread but the resulting String is then serialized across the method channel in one message — trigger: every `extractText` call, unbounded in page count and per-page text size (no cap on `pageCount` or text length) — consequence: peak memory spike proportional to 2× total PDF text plus channel-serialization cost; very large PDFs can hit the OS memory limit or take seconds to serialize — smallest safe fix: stream per-page results (return an array of page strings like `extractTextPerPage` does, or chunk the response) instead of building one joined String, and/or cap the returned text size.
- [medium] macos/Runner/PdfTextPlugin.swift:112-118 — extractTextPerPage builds the full per-page text array for all pages even when the caller only needs `hasAnyText` semantics for the error path — for an image-only scanned PDF with thousands of pages, every page's `page.string` extraction (PDFKit text extraction is the expensive part) runs to completion just to decide `hasAnyText`, and the `pages` array is still fully populated — trigger: every `extractTextPerPage` call on a text-free PDF, unbounded in page count — consequence: O(n) PDFKit text-extraction work (the dominant cost) executed even though the result is discarded for the NO_TEXT error path; large scans take noticeably longer than necessary — smallest safe fix: short-circuit — stop scanning once `hasAnyText` is false-provable is not possible, but for the error path only: extract lazily / return pages only when text exists, or early-exit the loop when a page has text and defer building the array until needed (note: for the success path the full array is required, so the fix is to skip text extraction entirely for pages once `hasAnyText` is true only if the caller doesn't need them — otherwise at minimum avoid `trimmingCharacters` allocation per page by using a cheaper emptiness check).
- [low] macos/Runner/PdfTextPlugin.swift:115 — `pageText.trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespacesAndNewlines)` allocates a new trimmed copy of every page's text per page just to test emptiness — for a 1000-page PDF with ~50KB text per page this is ~50MB of transient allocations churned per call — trigger: per page per `extractTextPerPage` call — consequence: allocation churn proportional to total text, adds GC/allocator pressure on the background queue — smallest safe fix: test emptiness without copying (e.g. check `pageText.range(of: "\\S", options: .regularExpression)` or iterate characters) or use `pageText.count > 0` plus a whitespace-only fast path.
- [info] macos/Runner/PdfTextPlugin.swift:150-156 — `checkEmbeddedText` correctly bounds work to the first 3 pages (`min(3, document.pageCount)`), so the OCR-vs-PDFKit decision is O(3) regardless of document size — no finding; noted as the bounded sibling of the unbounded loops above.
- [info] macos/Runner/MemoryMonitorPlugin.swift:36-47 — `getPhysicalFootprint` performs one mach `task_info` syscall per `getMemoryUsage` call; the handler runs synchronously on the platform (main) thread inside `handle(_:)` — trigger: every memory-monitor method-channel invocation (typically a periodic poll from Dart) — consequence: a single syscall is microseconds and does not block the main thread meaningfully; only a finding if the poll interval is sub-millisecond, which is not evidenced — no actionable defect at the shown call rate; noted for completeness.
