- [medium] macos/Runner/VisionOcrPlugin.swift:93-119 — per-page sequential OCR loop with no batching: each page is rendered (lines 104-108) then OCR'd synchronously via `Self.ocrImage` (line 111) inside a `for` loop over `pageCount`, and Vision `VNRecognizeTextRequest` with `recognitionLevel = .accurate` (lines 165-166) is CPU/GPU-heavy per page — a 100-page PDF serializes ~100 full-resolution renders + accurate-mode recognition runs on one thread, blocking the `DispatchQueue.global` worker for the entire document and delaying the single `result` callback (line 121) until every page completes; trigger = one `ocrPdf` method call per document, growing input = page count (unbounded — `pageCount` is read straight from the PDF with no cap, and the `scale` clamp at line 44 bounds only per-page bitmap size, not page count), work per unit = render-to-bitmap + accurate OCR per page, affected resource = background thread occupancy and user-visible latency of the method-channel reply — smallest safe fix: batch pages into a single `VNImageRequestHandler`/`VNRecognizeTextRequest` sequence or chunk the loop and stream partial results, and cap `pageCount` (e.g. process first N pages per call, paginate the rest).
- [medium] macos/Runner/VisionOcrPlugin.swift:100-101,132-160 — per-page bitmap allocation churn: `renderPage` allocates a fresh full-page `CGContext` (width×height at `scale` up to 4×, line 44) for every page in the loop and discards it after `makeImage()` (line 160), so a 100-page document at scale 4 allocates and frees ~100 large pixel buffers back-to-back — trigger = each iteration of the page loop, growing input = page count × scale (scale is clamped 0.5–4.0 but page count is unbounded), work per unit = one multi-MB context allocation + fill + draw + `makeImage` copy per page, affected resource = allocator pressure and transient memory spikes that can approach the jetsam threshold the scale clamp was meant to avoid — smallest safe fix: reuse a single pooled `CGContext`/bitmap across pages (or render at scale 1 and let Vision downsample) instead of allocating per page.
- [low] macos/Runner/VisionOcrPlugin.swift:182-184 — sort key recomputed per comparison: `observations.sorted` calls `boundingBox.origin.y` on both operands for every O(n log n) comparison instead of precomputing a decorated array of (y, observation) pairs once — trigger = every OCR result set (per page), growing input = number of text observations on the page (unbounded by page content density), work per unit = repeated property access + sort comparisons, affected resource = minor CPU on the OCR thread — smallest safe fix: map to `(y, observation)` tuples, sort once, then unwrap.
- [low] macos/Runner/VisionOcrPlugin.swift:187-193 — `topCandidates(1)` invoked inside the result loop when Vision already exposes the top candidate per observation, adding a per-line candidate-array allocation; trigger = per observation per page, growing input = observation count, work per unit = one array allocation + candidate lookup, affected resource = transient allocations on the OCR thread — smallest safe fix: use `observation.topCandidates(1).first` once per observation (already the shape) or the framework's first-candidate accessor if available; impact is marginal, fix only if profiling shows it.
- [info] macos/Runner/VisionOcrPlugin.swift:44 — the `scale` clamp bounds per-page bitmap dimensions but nothing bounds `pageCount` (line 89) or total rendered area, so the "defensive clamp" comment (lines 41-43) does not actually prevent the multi-hundred-MB cumulative allocation it describes for large documents — consequence: the jetsam risk the comment warns about is only mitigated per page, not per request — smallest safe fix: cap pages per `ocrPdf` call and paginate.

- [medium] lib/features/script_import/pdf_page_view.dart:59 — `_pageCache` is a plain map with no LRU ordering maintained on cache hits — `_renderPage`'s cache-hit path (lines 155–166) removes and re-inserts the hit page, which does refresh insertion order, but `_cachePut`'s eviction scan (lines 141–147) only evicts when length exceeds `_maxCachedPages` and never reorders on hit; consequence: with repeated flips between >4 pages the oldest-but-recently-viewed page can be evicted while a stale one lingers, causing repeated multi-hundred-ms re-renders and fresh multi-MB RGBA allocations per flip-back; smallest safe fix: on cache hit in `_cachePut`/`_renderPage`, re-insert the key (remove+add) so insertion order tracks recency before the eviction scan.
- [medium] lib/features/script_import/pdf_page_view.dart:141-147 — eviction scan allocates a fresh list (`toList()`) and re-checks `identical(_pageCache[k], _pageImage)` for every key on every put — work per put is O(cache size) with an allocation, triggered on every page render/flip; consequence: negligible at `_maxCachedPages = 4` today, but any raise of the cap or reuse of this pattern elsewhere scales linearly; smallest safe fix: track recency in insertion order and evict the single oldest non-on-screen key directly instead of materializing the full candidate list.
- [low] lib/features/script_import/pdf_page_view.dart:109-120 — `dispose` copies the whole cache (`_pageCache.values.toList()`) and defers disposal via `addPostFrameCallback` — allocation is O(cached pages) (≤4) and the deferred loop disposes each image on the UI thread in one frame; consequence: a burst of native `ui.Image` frees inside a single post-frame callback can jank one frame on low-end devices when all 4 cached pages are live at teardown; smallest safe fix: dispose images inside the same deferred callback but batch via `image.dispose()` in the existing loop is already minimal — acceptable as-is; no change needed (info-level observation only).
- [medium] lib/features/script_import/pdf_page_view.dart:261-262 — `_pageOcrCache` is a map keyed by page number with no eviction bound — every page the user views while `highlightText` is set adds a full OCR line list (`List<OcrPageLine>`) that is never removed; consequence: on a long rehearsal session paging through a 100+ page script, memory grows unboundedly with retained OCR results (each page's full line/word list), and the cache only grows; smallest safe fix: bound it like `_pageCache` (e.g. keep only the current page plus one neighbor, or cap at `_maxCachedPages` with LRU eviction).
- [low] lib/features/script_import/pdf_page_view.dart:283-295 — `OcrHighlightMatcher.locate(target, lines)` runs on every `_locateHighlight` completion and its result rects are stored, but the locate is re-run whenever `_locateHighlight` is re-invoked for the same page even when `_pageOcrCache[page]` hits (lines 261–283 reuse `lines` but still re-run `locate` and the debug log at 286–290); consequence: repeated flips back to the flagged page re-run text matching and re-log diagnostics each time — cheap per call, but redundant recomputation with repeating arguments; smallest safe fix: memoize the computed rects per page alongside the cached lines (e.g. cache `(lines, rects)` pairs) and skip re-locate when the rects for `(page, target)` already exist.
- [medium] lib/features/script_import/script_import_screen.dart:181-201 — `_countsFor` memoizes on list identity via `_previewCounts`, but `_openReview` (lines 437–452) replaces `_preview` with a NEW `ParsedScript` whose `lines` is `result.lines` — a fresh list instance — so the identity check at line 184 fails and the O(n) full-line scan (dialogue/review/notScript counting over every line, plus the switch per line) re-runs on every review-return rebuild; consequence: for a 500+ line script, each return from the review screen re-walks all lines during a build; smallest safe fix: key the memo on the `ParsedScript` instance (or a content hash/version counter) rather than list identity, or invalidate-and-recompute once per accepted review result which is the same cost — the real fix is ensuring the memo key survives the rebuild (e.g. store counts on the `ParsedScript` via Expando like `acts` in script_models.dart:406).
- [low] lib/features/script_import/script_import_screen.dart:238-254 — `...script.characters.map(...)` inside a `ListView(children: [...])` builds ALL character tiles eagerly (not lazy) — for a script with dozens of characters this constructs every ListTile/Avatar widget on every preview rebuild; consequence: rebuild cost grows with character count on each setState (dialect tap, save toggle); smallest safe fix: use `ListView.builder` with the character list as the item source so tiles are built on demand.
- [low] lib/features/script_import/script_import_screen.dart:262 — `...script.lines.take(30).map(...)` is bounded at 30 so no unbounded growth, but the surrounding `ListView` still constructs all 30 line-preview widgets (each a `RichText` with multiple `TextSpan`s) eagerly on every rebuild; consequence: 30 RichText constructions per setState is acceptable but wasteful when only a few are visible; smallest safe fix: same as above — `ListView.builder` over the combined children source.
- [low] lib/features/script_import/script_import_screen.dart:554-565 — `_colorForIndex` allocates nothing (const list) but is called per character tile and per dialogue line span on every rebuild; consequence: negligible; no finding warranted beyond noting it is already const — clean.
- [medium] lib/features/script_import/script_import_screen.dart:457-482 — `_recountCharacters` runs a full O(lines × multiCharacters) scan plus a sort on every review return (called from `_openReview` line 449 inside the setState that also rebuilds the whole preview); consequence: for large scripts this is O(n log n) work on the UI thread inside a build-triggering setState, janking the frame right after the review screen pops; smallest safe fix: hoist the recount into the review screen's result computation (compute once when the user confirms) or memoize per `(existing, result.lines)` pair.
- [low] lib/features/script_import/script_import_screen.dart:603-637 — `_commitStagedPdf` performs sequential awaits on independent file operations (`copy` → `rename` → `delete`); the copy→rename pair is inherently ordered (rename depends on the copy), but the staged-file `delete` (line 624) is independent of the rename completing and could run concurrently; consequence: one extra round-trip of disk latency per accept, only on user accept (rare trigger); smallest safe fix: `unawaited`/parallelize the delete with the rename, or leave as-is given the low frequency — borderline; reported at low for completeness of the sequential-await class.
- [low] lib/features/script_import/script_import_screen.dart:615-617 — `getApplicationDocumentsDirectory()` + `Directory.existsSync/createSync` run on every accept even when the directory already exists; `existsSync` is a synchronous stat on the UI isolate; consequence: one blocking stat per accept — negligible frequency (user accept only); smallest safe fix: none needed; clean at this scale.
- [info] lib/features/script_import/pdf_page_view.dart:176-179 — `Pdfrx.getCacheDirectory ??=` assigns a closure that calls `getTemporaryDirectory()` on every invocation of the getter if the framework calls it repeatedly; consequence: each call re-resolves the temp dir path via a platform channel instead of caching the resolved string; smallest safe fix: memoize the resolved path inside the closure (`late String? _tmpDir` pattern) so the platform channel is hit once.

Let me analyze the inlined file `lib/data/services/script_parser.dart` (1802 lines) for performance issues.

Key observations:

1. **`_detectTitleHeaders` (lines 221-347)**: This method is called from `parse()` at line 146. Inside it:
   - Line 241: `rawText.split('\n')` — creates a list of all lines.
   - Lines 262-294: loop over all lines with multiple regex checks per line (`RegExp(r'[.!?]$')` at 266, `RegExp(r'^(ACT|SCENE|...')` at 267-269, `RegExp(r'^\d+\s+|\s+\d+$')` at 278, 280). These regexes are constructed **inside the loop** — per-line RegExp compilation. That's a "Regex compiled per call/iteration" finding (high per checklist).
   - Lines 329-331: `rawText.split('\n')` again inside the title-seeded path — re-splits the whole text (redundant recomputation; the `allLines` list from line 241 is in scope but not reused). Also `_normalizeForHeader(l)` per line — O(n) work repeated.
   - `isCharacterName` (lines 225-231): called inside the candidate filter at line 313 — for each candidate norm, it loops over `knownCharacters` and calls `_normalizeForHeader` and `_normalizeCharacter` per character. `_normalizeForHeader` does regex replaceAll per call. If `knownCharacters` is large (hundreds of characters in a full play), and candidates are up to ~60-char lines... Actually candidates are `counts.entries` — bounded by distinct normalized header lines. The work is candidates × characters × regex work. This is nested iteration but bounded by candidate count (≤ distinct norms) × character count. Could be O(n·m). Worth a medium finding: membership test on a set would be better, but normalization makes it non-trivial. Actually the checklist says "Membership test on a list inside a loop (Python)" — this is Dart, but the shape is the same: linear scan over `knownCharacters` (a Set, but iterated as a list) inside the candidate filter loop, with regex normalization per element. Medium.

2. **`_detectCharacters` family**: `_detectCharactersStandard` (554-572) runs `pattern.allMatches(rawText)` — regex over the whole text. That's fine (single pass). But `_detectCharactersTitleCase` (632-655) — `pattern.allMatches(rawText)` then counts. Fine.

3. **`_mergeOcrCharacterNames` (817-923)**: 
   - Lines 825-830: for each known character, builds a RegExp `'^$escaped\\.\\s'` **per character** and runs `allMatches(rawText)` over the entire raw text. That's C regex scans × character count over the full text — O(characters × text length). For a full-length play with ~50-100 characters, that's 50-100 full-text regex scans. This is per-item work over the whole corpus — high-ish. Trigger: once per parse (import), so it's import-time, not per-request. Severity: it's a one-time import cost, but a full-length script import is the hot path (user-triggered import). The checklist "Regex compiled per call/iteration" — compiled per character here. And "Per-item remote I/O that could batch" — not I/O but CPU. I'd rate medium-high. Given trigger frequency = once per import (user-initiated), and work = full-text regex scan per character (N characters), consequence: import latency grows linearly with character count × text size. Medium.
   - Lines 854-895: fuzzy match loop — `nameList` × `nameList` nested iteration with `_editDistance` (Levenshtein, O(len²) per pair) inside. That's O(n² · L²) where n = character count, L = name length. For n=100 characters with ~10-char names, that's 100×100×100 = 10^6 char comparisons — fine actually. But `_editDistance` allocates two lists per call (`List.generate(lb+1)` and `List.filled(lb+1)`) — allocation churn inside the nested loop. For n=100, 10^4 pairs × 2 allocations of ~11 elements — small. But the nested loop is O(n²) with edit distance inside — for large casts (n=200+), 40k pairs × O(100) = 4M ops. Medium. Also note: the loop at 870 iterates ALL candidates for each name even after `best` found — no early break when `bestDist` can't improve... actually it keeps searching for better candidates, which is intentional (closest candidate). But there's no `break` when dist == 0... `if (dist == 0 || dist > maxDist) continue;` — dist==0 means identical, skipped. OK.
   - Also `_isConflictingTitleVariant` calls `_stripTitle` twice per pair (lines 877, 1115-1116) — each does `name.toUpperCase()` and a 15-prefix loop. Inside the O(n²) loop. Adds constant factor.

4. **`_resolveTitleCaseAbbreviations` (929-982)**:
   - Lines 933-943: nested loop over `byLength` — O(n²) with `startsWith` — fine for small n.
   - Lines 960-981: for each abbrev, loops over `fullNames` — O(n × m). `fullNames` is a Set built from stage directions — could be large. Bounded.

5. **`_detectMultiCharacterNames` (987-1018)**: for each known character, calls `_tryMultiCharacterSplit` and `_tryGluedMultiCharacterSplit`. The latter (1024-1077) iterates over ALL `knownCharacters` per name — O(n²) — and inside, `_editDistance` per candidate suffix pair. Plus at line 1007: `RegExp('^$escaped\\.\\s')` compiled per name and `allMatches(rawText)` full-text scan per name — same shape as `_mergeOcrCharacterNames` counts. O(n full-text scans). Medium.

   Wait — `_tryGluedMultiCharacterSplit` is called for every name in `knownCharacters` (line 1004), and inside it iterates `knownCharacters` (line 1035) — O(n²) pairs, each with up to 2 `_editDistance` calls (line 1051) of O(L²) work and list allocations. For n=100: 10^4 pairs × ~2×100 ops = 2M — OK-ish, but allocation churn: `_editDistance` allocates 2 lists per call → 2×10^4 allocations. Medium-low. Actually the bigger issue: `_tryGluedMultiCharacterSplit` returns early only via `return [prefixKnown, known]` — but the outer loop at 1035 has no early exit when a match is found mid-way... it returns on first `bestPrefix != null` — wait, no: it computes `bestPrefix` for a given `known`, and if non-null, returns immediately. So per name, it scans knowns until first hit. Worst case O(n) per name → O(n²) total. Fine to report as medium given import-time only.

- [medium] lib/features/auth/auth_screen.dart:312-367 — `_submit` awaits `SupabaseService.instance.signUpWithEmail`/`signInWithEmail` (network round-trips) on the UI isolate with no timeout or cancellation; a slow/hung auth endpoint leaves the screen in `_isLoading` state indefinitely and blocks the user from retrying (button disabled at 257/221) — trigger: every sign-in/sign-up tap; input: unbounded network latency; work: one full HTTP round-trip per attempt; affected resource: main isolate + user-facing auth flow; consequence: auth screen appears frozen, spinner runs forever on stalled network; fix: wrap the awaits with a `Timeout`/`Future.any` deadline (e.g. 15s) and surface a retryable error in `finally`.
- [medium] lib/features/auth/auth_screen.dart:389-409 — `_resendConfirmation` performs a per-tap network resend (`client.auth.resend`) with no debounce/rate-limit and no client-side cap; repeated taps (button re-enabled in `finally` at 408) each spawn a fresh HTTP request and a new SnackBar, so a user mashing "Resend email" issues unbounded sequential requests — trigger: each tap; input: unbounded tap count; work: one network call + one snackbar per tap; affected resource: auth endpoint + UI; consequence: request storm to the auth API and stacked snackbars; fix: debounce/disable for N seconds after a successful resend (e.g. a 30s cooldown timer) before re-enabling the button.
- [low] lib/features/auth/auth_screen.dart:372-387 — `_friendlyAuthError` calls `e.toString().toLowerCase()` on every error and then string-`contains` over the whole message; for large exception payloads (e.g. a full HTTP body embedded in the message) this allocates a lowercased copy per error event — trigger: each failed auth attempt; input: error message length (unbounded from server); work: O(n) copy + up to 4 substring scans; affected resource: transient allocations on the UI thread; consequence: minor GC churn per failed attempt, negligible at typical message sizes; fix: match on a short prefix/substring of the raw message instead of lowercasing the entire payload, or cap the string before scanning.
- [info] lib/features/auth/auth_screen.dart:421 — `_skipAuth` writes `auth_skipped` via `sharedPreferencesProvider` synchronously in the build-path callback with no error handling; if the underlying SharedPreferences write is async/failing (assumed — the provider's implementation is not inlined), the flag may not persist, contradicting the stated intent that the user "isn't asked again on next launch" — trigger: each skip tap; consequence: possible re-prompt on next launch; fix: await the write and surface/log failure. (Assumption: provider wraps `SharedPreferences.setBool`; not visible in this batch.)
